ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u19	Thank you very much. Another question was raised by Mr Grzegorz Furgo of the Modern, on the impact of local governments on the management of maritime ports - to the Minister for Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. I ask you to ask me a question.

The free transfer to regional governments and port cities of significant holdings of State Treasury shares in companies managing seaports of fundamental importance to the economy would, in our opinion, increase the economic efficiency of these companies, as well as ensure a more dynamic development of port cities and regions. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u21	Thank you. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation by Minister Paweł Brzezicki.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u22	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, The legal act which regulates the operation of Polish ports is the Act of 20 December 1996 on ports and seaports. Municipalities where ports of primary importance for the national economy are located are actively involved in the management of these ports. Art. 13 of the Act will determine the participation of port municipalities in companies managing ports of primary importance for the national economy, which should be at least 31% respectively in Gdańsk, Gdynia at least 34%, Świnoujście and Szczecin at 24.5%. In practice, the cities and municipalities did not take up these shares and today it is as follows: in the company of the Management Board of the Maritime Port of Gdańsk SA the municipality of Gdańsk - 2,31 , in the company of the Management Board of the Maritime Port Gdynia SA municipality Gdynia - 0.0436% , in the company of the Management Board of Maritime Ports Szczecin and Świnoujście SA municipality Szczecin - 0,52% , Świnoujście Municipality - 0,15% . The participation or capital injection in such a low percentage is in a huge discrepancy with the real impact of municipalities on the management of in-plus ports. The Law on ports and marinas provides for specific powers for the municipalities concerned regardless of their participation in the ownership structure. On this basis, the municipalities are entitled to indicate their representatives to the composition of the supervisory board, e.g. Gdańsk - 3 representatives, Gdynia - 4 representatives, Szczecin - 2, Świnoujście - 2. At the same time, the Act ensures that representatives of the municipalities are each chair of the supervisory boards, i.e. they decide on corporate decisions within the supervisory board. Experience so far shows that there are levels of potential conflicts of interest between municipalities and port management companies. In particular, it concerns issues relating to the levying of property taxes and charges for perpetual use or the development of areas within the administrative boundaries of ports. The latter is particularly important from the point of view of infrastructure investments in ports and is seen as a barrier to investment in port infrastructure. Port managers manage only part of the port area, which shows that the municipalities which are shareholders of the companies have not contributed land to them since the date of their establishment, although the municipalities have land located within seaports, often of significant size. Mr President, this is the current situation. However, what the economics science says about port management, and what we have witnessed by building this law until 1996, indicates that the current structure is a structure that is too dimensioned under the real needs of municipalities. They're not even fulfilling what they can achieve capital today. Thank you very much. [[Thank you very much.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u26	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! National Day of Remembrance, Soldiers of the Cursed, solid soldiers provides a unique opportunity to specialize in honoring the last veterans of the association Freedom and Independence, Home Army, insurgents of Warsaw and other anti-communist formations related to the continuity of the fight for their homeland, the promotion of the war's achievements of soldiers so-called. the second conspiracy and formation based on the boastful patterns of the patriotic attitudes of the army and society as a whole. At this point, I can fully sign on behalf of the Ministry of National Defence and my own under the diagnosis and description of the situation just presented by the Member. The memory day, celebrated for the fifth time this year, prompts us to reflect on the difficult and tragic events of our history. It is a reason for me to be proud and personally satisfied that the Polish Armed Forces will be able to actively engage in the implementation of projects to commemorate and raise the profile of these events from the past. I'm relaying information about the Ministry of National Defence's intentions to specially honor the fallen heroes and the last veterans of the anti-communist uprising. February 22nd this year A press conference took place at the Polish Army Museum, during which the Minister of National Defence signed a decision on the celebration of the National Day of Remembrance, Soldiers of the Cursed, solid soldiers. According to this document, the soldiers' graves and the surroundings of memorial sites dedicated to them will be organized as part of the action under the slogan "Soldier's Memory." There will be ceremonial calls and gatherings throughout the country in military units, during which the Minister's message to the soldiers will be read on this day. There will also be organized ceremonies of flower folding and lighting on the graves of these soldiers and at memorial sites dedicated to them. During the same press conference, a prototype of uniformed soldiers' uniform was presented together with a presentation of the concept of introducing a special, approved in the future by official and formal uniform for veterans fighting for independence of the Republic of Poland. Our intention is to recreate the forms of special honoring of these heroes, following the actions taken by the independent Republic in 1919 against the last surviving January insurgents. We strive to ensure that veterans are properly cared for by the state, and to emphasize that the Polish state recognizes them and honors them as soldiers of the army, the Polish Army of course, by among others. Allowing soldiers' uniforms and military honors to be given to them by modern soldiers. On February 29th, the next Monday, the Polish Theatre will host a festive concert about the Enchanted Soldiers. The concert will be preceded by the ceremony of the award of the Honorary Badge and Medals of the Commemorative Custos of Tradition, Glory and Fame of the Polish Armed Forces and the acts of appointment for the next military degrees for the families of the 23 steadfast soldiers who fell for their homeland. In addition, the decision to appoint 8 other veterans under independence for further military degrees is being examined. On the occasion of this day, the Ministry of National Defence organizes the ceremony on 1 March. A mass will be celebrated in the field cathedral, and a solemn appeal of memory with the participation of the Minister of National Defence will be held in the courtyard of the Polish Military Museum. Then there will be a march before the tomb of the Unknown Soldier, where the solemn laying of the wreaths will take place. I would also like to stress that the action initiated by the order of the Minister of National Defence is to cover all military units throughout the country and thus become visible and to be remembered not only by soldiers but also by all local communities associated with these units. I also wanted to emphasise, in relation to Mr Kurzapa's words, that we want this part of the military tradition to become a basis for the formation of the military tradition of the Polish army today and in the future. And in this spirit, and in this direction, we will conduct training both in military units and in officers' and petty schools. The programme will be developed in this academic year so that it can be implemented from autumn, September and October. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u48	Ladies and gentlemen! The investment arbitration case is indeed one of the most important issues in terms of protecting the interests of the State Treasury. 11 such proceedings are currently under way. The value of the dispute in these cases is over PLN 4 billion, PLN 4100 million, EUR 1 billion, USD 100 million, which is between PLN 8 and PLN 9 billion, depending on the rate of the euro and the dollar. These are indeed actions in which the threat is quite important, although so far we are winning these cases. On the other hand, one must be aware that each of these proceedings, even winnings, involves costs on the part of the State Treasury in the order of PLN 3/4 million. It is not always possible to recover these costs from foreign investors suing the Polish State. Moreover, it is not a rule either. Therefore, it seems necessary not only to terminate those contracts but also to bring about the loss of legal force as soon as possible, i.e. shortening the transitional period for which these contracts will continue to apply after termination. Therefore, it was decided, in the direction of the Ministry of the Treasury, that, of course, after any analysis of the profits and losses associated with this process, these contracts should be terminated as soon as possible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u52	The main reason why mutual investment agreements exist is to provide investors with a guarantee that the State will not arbitraryly enter into their powers, and this is an expression of a limited confidence in the justice system of the country in which investors invest. In our view, and for this reason these agreements were concluded so as to encourage in the 1990s, because it was also a process that started in the 1990s, and for this reason it was a guarantee that investors would not depend on certain political conditions, in our view we have already achieved this degree of democratic development, that the courts guarantee that the case is recognised in a manner independent of political influence, and therefore there is no reason to maintain the favour of these investors. As for the number, because I have not yet responded to the previous answer, that is 60 mutual investment agreements. I do not think that the procedure, the transfer of the case to legal proceedings will have a negative impact on the powers of investors, but certainly more legal certainty will be ensured, because investors, like all other entrepreneurs operating in Poland, will be judged, their claims will be judged according to clearly defined legal standards, and this is not always the case in the case of investment arbitration. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u55	Thank you very much. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Treasury, Mr Henryk Baranowski, answers the question. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u56	Dear Marshal! Dear Gentlemen and Mr. Messengers, The primary competence of the Minister of Treasury is the exercise of rights from shares, shares of companies with the participation of the Treasury. In the portfolio of assets supervised by the Minister are companies of importance for the functioning of the country's economy and our energy security. Among them are mainly companies operating in the natural gas and oil sector, such as Polish Petroleum Mining and Gas Industry, Lotus Group and Polish Petroleum Corporation Orlen. The first two entities mentioned by me are companies in which the Treasury has a majority in the shareholder structure. This is respectively the case with PGNiG over 70% , whereas in the case of the Lotus Group more than half of the capital structure. In the third of the companies mentioned above, i.e. in the Orlen Group, the share of the State Treasury is minority and stands at 27%. Turning to the synthetic discussion of the activities of each of these entities, it should be emphasized that Polskie Górnictwo Naftowe i Gazowiectwo is the largest company active on the Polish market in the exploration and extraction of natural gas and its trading. The PGNiG Capital Group has a leading position in all areas of the gas sector in the country, i.e. in the area of natural gas imports, exploration of deposits, extraction of natural gas and oil, storage of gas fuels and sale of natural gas. According to the statutes, the company performs tasks to ensure the country's energy security in terms of continuity of gas supply to its customers and to maintain the necessary reserves in the safe operation of gas networks, balancing the balance of gas fuels. Annually, the company brings about 10 billion m3 of gas to the country, representing about 2/3 of the gas consumed in Poland. At the same time, the company is producing more than 4 billion m3. This makes the company the main importer of natural gas to Poland and the largest producer of the above mentioned raw material in Poland. It should also be stressed that PGNiG is one of the largest heat producers and the only entity obliged to maintain stocks of mandatory natural gas, the eighth producer of electricity in the country in terms of the volume produced and the owner of the gas distribution network and seven national high methane gas warehouses. Thus, PGNiG is the fundamental pillar on which the energy security of our country is based. In turn, the Lotos Group together with companies belonging to the capital group is a vertically integrated oil company engaged in the extraction and processing of crude oil and wholesale and retail sales of petroleum products. The main activity of the Lotus Group is the in-depth processing of raw materials towards fuel production, the trade in high-margin petroleum products and the exploration and extraction of hydrocarbons. By Lotos Petrobaltic, Lotos Exploration & Production Norge, the company conducts exploration and production work in the field of oil operations in the Baltic Sea and in the area of the Norwegian Continental Shell. By AB Lotos Geonafta, the company also has access to land-based hydrocarbon deposits located in Lithuania. In 2014, the company held a 33% share of the domestic fuel market. In addition, it should be noted that the Lotos Group is a market leader in the production of motor oils reaching 45 countries and has a leading position on the domestic road asphalt market. At the same time, the company manages a network of 450 fuel stations across the country, which operates 17 so-called. passengers' places on motorways and express roads. As far as the Polish Petroleum Corporation Orlen is concerned, together with the companies accompanying the Orlen Group, it is a leading producer and distributor of refinery and petrochemical products in the region. PKN Orlen is also carrying out projects in the mining and energy segment. The Orlen Group manages a complex of seven refineries, three of which are located in Poland, three in the Czech Republic and one in Lithuania. Refineries in Płock and in the Czech Republic are integrated into the petrochemical activity of the Orlen Group, which allows to achieve numerous effects of cost and operational synergies. It should also be added that the Orlen Group has the largest retail network in Europe with more than 2,700 fuel stations located in Poland, Germany, the Czech Republic and Lithuania. The refinery in Płock processed 14 million tonnes of crude oil in 2014. The combined share of PKN Orlen in the domestic fuel market reached 60% , whereas in the retail market it is 37%. The Orlen Group is involved in oil and gas exploration projects. In 2013, the Orlen Group started its international expansion in the exploration and extraction sector by acquiring shares of the Canadian exploration and production company TriOil Resources. At the same time, I kindly inform you that the role of the Minister of the Treasury as an active shareholder of companies with the participation of the Treasury is to support all actions undertaken by supervised entities which should, as a rule, lead to an increase in their value. The State Treasury Minister, as the authority responsible for its supervision, focuses on building the value of the companies in which he owns his holdings, assuming the implementation of the State's energy policy. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The aim of this policy, in our view, is to secure the implementation of strategic tasks in the natural gas and oil sector. However, it should be stressed that the initiative of all kinds of consolidation processes should come from stakeholders and their shareholders and should be justified by the expected synergies, the positive effects on the labour market and the support of local communities. Regardless of the above, in replying directly to the question asked, I kindly inform you that the Minister of the Treasury is currently not carrying out any analytical work on the possible consolidation of State Treasury companies in the supervision of oil and gas SMEs. At the same time, it should be emphasized that in the context of ensuring the energy security of Poland it is necessary to maintain the holding of the State Treasury shares in PGNiG, PKN Orlen and the Lotos Group at least at a level that allows to maintain the corporate power of the State Treasury. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u61	Thank you very much. The question is answered by the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance, Mr Wiesław Jasiński. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u62	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! In response to the current question of 23 February 2016, asked by Mrs Joanna Kopcińska, Mr Piotr Uściński and Mr Paweł Lisiecki from the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, on the measures to assist Polish entrepreneurs associated with the Agreement for entrepreneurs who are victims of labour outsourcing by the Polish authorities in the context of enforcement proceedings conducted by the tax authorities, I kindly present the position of the Minister of Finance in this case. The Minister of Finance notes that the problem of entrepreneurs using employee outsourcing institutions is of a broader nature and concerns both tax and social insurance claims, with enforcement proceedings being carried out against economic operators as the last stage of the collection process, resulting from irregularities found in the fulfilment of the obligations laid down in the various laws. I agree with and share her concern. The statistics are what you have presented - this is about 500 entrepreneurs, more than 20,000. employees, most of these entities are present in the Podkarpackie Voivodship. At the moment there are 26 proceedings before the provincial administrative courts, in four cases the decision was given. In any event, taxpayers' complaints have been dismissed. In seven cases, the Minister of Finance took advantage of the possibility of granting relief to these taxpayers. It should be stressed that the tax authorities responsible for implementing the revenue to the State budget cannot overlook and accept the existence of irregularities in the payment of due tax obligations. The selective treatment of taxpayers would be incompatible with the principle of equality and therefore socially unjustified. Each entrepreneur, when making certain business decisions, should be aware of both the potential benefits and the risks associated with those decisions. However, in view of the need for support for injured entrepreneurs and the fact that decisions which have so far taken place in individual cases before administrative courts have not yet set up a clear and sustainable judicial line, the Minister of Finance has taken action to avoid the situation in which entrepreneurs are brought to bankruptcy and the job deprivation of the thousands of workers referred to by the Member. It was therefore considered appropriate that, at this stage, administrative courts should not take enforcement action against those taxable persons which could lead to the risk of bankruptcy or liquidation of their businesses until the final settlement of disputes before administrative courts in the application of the employment outsourcing institutions. Such recommendations were made by the Minister of Finance to the heads of the tax offices as enforcement bodies. On the other hand, with regard to the enforcement proceedings already under way, the Minister of Finance proposed to the directors of the tax chambers as supervisory authorities to consider the possibility for the institution to suspend ex officio enforcement proceedings in accordance with its statutory powers. However, it should be stressed that an assessment of the appropriateness of carrying out or stopping enforcement proceedings should be carried out separately in each individual case by the competent authorities, i.e. the heads of the tax offices or the directors of the tax chambers respectively. At the same time, I would like to clarify that the legislation in force does not allow the Finance Minister to take action on individual, specific taxpayers' matters and to instruct subordinate individuals to deal with them. However, I would like to point out that apart from the institutions, the suspension of enforcement proceedings by means of an instrument of support for taxpayers who are in temporary financial difficulties are reliefs in the payment of tax liabilities as regulated by the Tax Order. At any stage of enforcement proceedings, the trader may therefore request such relief, e.g. on the distribution of entitlements into instalments. On the other hand, in the event of the distribution of debts into instalments, enforcement proceedings shall be suspended ex officio. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Therefore, in answer to the question asked, I explain that the tax authorities and administrative enforcement authorities, while complying with the legislation, are also required to assess individually the measures applied to taxpayers. Many entrepreneurs cooperate with tax and enforcement authorities, communicating on the deadline for payment of the amounts in question, while waiting for the decisions of the competent administrative courts. Any illegal activity should, however, be reported to the competent directors of the tax chambers as the supervisory authorities of the tax and enforcement authorities. For its part, the Minister of Finance will also endeavour to reduce the risk of bankruptcy or the liquidation of legally active entrepreneurs as a result of unjustified actions by government administrations. As you probably know, the District Attorney's Office in Wrocław also conducts preliminary proceedings, criminal proceedings. [[♪ The bell ♪]] They will also bring a lot to the issues which remain the concern of the Member. Thank you very much.

They were about something else, namely the implementation of the agreement between Poland and the Russian Federation concluded in 1996. To this agreement every year, sometimes every longer period, it is necessary to include protocols which specify the amount of permits exchanged between the two parties in their transport relations. The expiry date of the last protocol, which was concluded in Sochi 3 years ago, passed at the end of the year and as far as I know, because I examined the information that I found in the Ministry of Infrastructure and Construction - at the end of that period Minister Paweł Olszewski was responsible - you could have concluded this agreement, you could have signed this protocol and you could have just dealt with it. That was in your jurisdiction. You've had a few months to do this. That was your problem, that was your job that you simply didn't do, sir. You left us this job, we did this job, and we did it brilliantly. I've never had so many congratulations in my life, so many praises that we've managed to solve such a difficult case. What was it about? To make it clear to everyone here, and to explain: The Russian Federation - and they, the Russians - do not hide it at all - intends to limit foreign transport or even eliminate it from their territory, because it is unable to cope with these market methods, their carriers are not able to provide this transport for various reasons, I will not get into it, because this is the thing Not for this place and not for this time. Therefore, the Russian Federation issued a law in February last year on the control of international transport in its territory. This bill actually got its power back in November. In December, the Russian Federation issued a regulation to this act which radically impeded the access of Polish carriers to this market. Nor will I go into the technical details of these regulations. However, the impact would be that, as experts estimate, about 85% Polish transport would be eliminated from the Russian market. Moreover, this would also make it difficult to access Polish goods to the Russian market, because the Russians interpret it in such a way that these goods and products which were manufactured in Poland, in the area of the Republic, but in factories belonging not to Poles, but to international companies or to foreign capital at all, will be interpreted as a transport from third countries. So this area of transport, which the Russian Federation wants to limit to the maximum. It wants to limit them to the maximum because it considers that if they are goods intended for transport to Russia, they should be transported by Russian carriers. And we've been discussing this for five rounds of very difficult negotiations. Very difficult, because we have made it clear from the beginning of the Russian side: we will start discussing the number of permits only when we establish the rules on which these transports will be carried out. It really took us a long time. But if we agreed to what the Russian side proposed, after a few months there would be no Polish transport on Russian roads. There would be no Polish transport on Russian roads. And we were fully aware of that as the management of the Ministry, but above all, I stress this, the carriers whose representatives were with us from the first to the last minute of negotiations and participated in all stages of these negotiations. This is not enough, on the final protocol initialled in Moscow, there are signatures of all representatives of the transport environment. That's what it looks like. That's the truth about what we've done. Ladies and gentlemen, you have mentioned that this agreement is temporary. Yeah, it's temporary. I did so with full awareness. Why? Because the Russian party claimed as follows: we cannot change our laws in one, two or three days. And it was difficult for them not to agree actually, given the different formulas of Russian law, according to which each regulation, although issued by some resort, concerning specific competences, in this case transport, must still be approved by the Ministry of Justice of the Russian Federation, which has its pace, which works without a deadline. And we accepted that argument. But at the same time, in order to secure our interests, we said: Okay, we date, we give two months, until April 15th, but by April 15th we also give important permits. After April 15th, the permits we've exchanged now will just be irrelevant. And if, by then, the Russian party fails to fulfil the obligations set out in the document signed in Moscow - I stress, the commitments to amend the regulations in a spirit which was also included in the agreement - unfortunately, the permits will expire. But here it's all on the Russian side now. Ladies and gentlemen, I would like to refer to one thread that you have been gracious to bring to the debate, namely the fuel thread. About the fact that the Russian side in every conversation raises the topic of the so-called. full tanks, i.e. the desire to refuel to full, in case you enter Poland, it is nothing new. In all prognosis it's raised, in everyone it's spoken of. And this time, too, this thread has repeatedly appeared during these five rounds of conversations. During the last tour in Moscow, we talked about this when we came to an agreement. The Russians have said that they are giving way very seriously because they are committed to changing their laws, and we are not giving up on anything. And of course you could insist, you could say: let's move away from the table, so let's for the next two weeks, 10 days, and maybe a month there will still be no transport between Poland and the Russian Federation. Ladies and gentlemen, it was possible to do this, but it was because of our responsibility that we did it differently. I said this: well, we will talk about this with the Minister of Finance of the Republic of Poland. And that's how it was written. Indeed, the Ministry of Infrastructure will apply to the Ministry of Finance to examine the matter to consider it. And what else am I gonna say? Well, we've agreed that this case, so it's written in the record, [[♪ The bell ♪]] It will only enter into the agenda for further talks when we no longer discuss this protocol, which I hope will be signed by mid-April or earlier, but we will talk about a new agreement between Poland and the Russian Federation. Only then. Moreover, ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to remind you that today's agreement is about 200 litres of fuel, which Russian carriers can bring to Poland, crossing the border, while in recent years, and this was primarily the decisions taken by the previous government, this limit has been increased from 200 l to 600 l. And it wasn't the decisions of law and justice. That's what I'd like to end with. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u115	Position of the PO Parliamentary Club on the draft law amending the Investment Funds Act and certain other laws, print No 266. During the legislative process on draft laws on: - amendment of the Investment Funds Act and certain other laws - print No 69, - amending the Act on Public Offering and the Conditions for Introducing Financial Instruments to Organized Trading, and on Public Companies and Certain Other Acts - print No. 70 decided to merge these projects into a single draft Act amending the Act on Investment Funds and some other laws. The draft laws aim to implement directives of the European Parliament and of the Council into the Polish legal system. They address the following issues: - managers of alternative investment funds, - the activities and supervision of institutions for occupational retirement provision, - coordination of laws, regulations and administrative provisions relating to undertakings for collective investment in transferable securities, - harmonisation of transparency requirements for information about issuers whose securities are admitted to trading on a regulated market, - a prospectus published in connection with a public offer or admission to trading of securities, - coordination of laws, regulations and administrative provisions relating to undertakings for collective investment in transferable securities as regards the functions of depositary, remuneration policy and sanctions. The draft laws have been prepared by the PO-PSL government and have been subject to a broad public consultation since the beginning of 2015. As a result of the consultation, good draft laws have been prepared which adapt difficult EU rules to Polish law. The current government adopted the vast majority of the regulations contained in these projects and directed them to the marshal's staff. The purpose of the AIFM Directive is to establish an internal market for alternative investment fund managers, i.e. managers of all types of funds which are not covered by the Directive. The most important decisions contained in the project are to determine the scope of the entities to which the regulations required by the AIFM Directive will apply. The rules do not create new forms of legal activity, but include in terms of entity the forms of activity covered by the AIFM Directive that are identified on the national market. The Act sets out the conditions and organisational requirements for AIFMs acting on the basis of authorisation, including the obligation to develop remuneration policies, to prevent conflicts of interest, to protect information and to manage risk. In general, the Public Offering Directive aims to improve the effectiveness of the existing transparency system, in particular as regards disclosure of information on the ownership of the company. It also strengthens the system of penalties and administrative measures for infringements of the directive, which will positively increase the safety of trading on the capital market and, very importantly, protect investors. At this point it should be stressed that the proposed system of administrative penalties and sanctions, and in particular their amount of up to 8 million PLN, met with great criticism. It was rightly argued that the level of the proposed penalties, although maximum, is not adequate to the conditions of the Polish capital market. It does not correspond to the size of companies listed on the Warsaw Stock Exchange. This would pose a real risk of reducing the competitiveness of the Polish capital market and reducing it. The PO Club has been in favour of significantly reducing penalties and adapting them to the reality of the Polish capital market since the beginning. It therefore welcomes the changes made in this area. The lower amounts of penalties proposed are still severe under Polish conditions and will be an effective and effective instrument to counter the violation of the law. To conclude, the Parliamentary Club supports the bill and will vote in favour of its adoption.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u116	The draft law on amending the Investment Funds Act and some other laws was a chance to create attractive conditions for investment funds in Poland. It could be the basis for a new competitive formula to invest in innovative ideas in our country. Unfortunately, as lately, there has been a lack of imagination, desire, or perhaps at the end of time. The proposed rules will implement in Polish law in principle only EU regulations, while at the same time they could significantly regulate the domestic market of investment funds. The Modern Posel Club agrees with the need to implement directives of the European Parliament and of the Council, but opposes not taking advantage of the opportunity to create better investment conditions in our country. The effect of the amendment should be to stimulate alternative sources of funding to banks, especially for small and medium-sized enterprises, to expand investment offers and to stimulate competition on the domestic capital market. We have suggested, for example, adding the possibility of investing within the family office, but this simple amendment was rejected by the committee. Or just because she was reported by the opposition? In conclusion, the law introduced should have attracted, not deterred professional investors from investing in Poland, who currently do not choose to be present in over-regulated markets (so-called. overegulated markets). As I mentioned, the effect of the amendment of the law should be to expand the investment offer and, while ensuring an adequate level of security, to stimulate competition on the domestic capital market, which would increase the innovation of the Polish economy. Despite this, the Posel Club of Modern Poland will vote in favour of the proposed project, but we do it out of concern for Poland, because of the need to adapt our law, not because of the real admiration of the draft law.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u117	High Izbo! Mr. Marshal! On 1 December 2015 two projects were submitted to the Polish Parliament: - Government draft law amending the Investment Funds Act and certain other laws - print No 69, - Government bill amending the Act on Public Offering and Conditions of Introduction of Financial Instruments to Organized Trading, and on Public Companies and Certain Other Acts - print No 70. On 28 January 2016, a resolution was adopted on the Committee on Public Finance to consider both projects jointly. On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I analyse the above laws on the agenda. The Investment Funds and Alternative Investment Fund Management Act sets out rules for the operation of financial institutions managing these funds in Poland, established in a country or a country of the European Union or outside. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 1a 2 identify entities whose activities are not alternative investment fund management. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 3 (1) (a) 4 the formula for the activity of investment funds on the Polish market is defined, i.e. we distinguish: 1. investment funds open; 2) Alternative investment funds: (a) an open specialised investment fund, (b) an investment fund closed. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: The tasks of the companies and managers of an alternative investment company and the depositaries respectively have been defined. They are to act independently and in the interests of the participants in the investment fund and investors of the alternative investment company respectively. I'm going to quote the article anyway. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1. The subject matter of the company is solely the creation, management and representation of open investment funds or foreign funds, including intermediation in the sale and redemption of units, and the management of a collective portfolio of securities. Art. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation That's right. With the permission of the Commission, the company may extend its activities to the creation of specialised open investment funds and closed investment funds, to the management of these funds, (...), for example, to intermediaries in the sale and redemption of units. The Act identifies specific situations where the Financial Supervision Commission may inter alia. require the fund to change its external valuer. The Commission may also revoke the authorisation to manage funds, etc. The entities operating on the Polish market must conclude a civil liability insurance contract for damage caused by failure or improper performance of their obligations related to the management of funds. The content of the draft law was consulted and consulted with the most important state, public, social and private institutions. The Act proposes the addition of provisions to implement undesirable behaviour related to the operation of investment funds and lists or amends certain types of offences which have been supplemented or modified by the signs of infringement set out in the Order or prohibition of proceedings, as pointed out in its position of 22 December 2015 by the Attorney General. A significant number of new draft implementing acts for this project should be indicated (Print No 69, part. 2.: The Minister of Justice defines, among others, how to keep the register of investment funds. The Finance Minister's regulation sets out the modalities and conditions for the conduct of intermediaries in the sale and redemption of units and titles of participation, as well as investment advice in relation to such instruments. The Minister of Finance shall determine the requirements for the development of remuneration policies in association with investment funds. The same Minister shall also determine the manner, mode and conditions of operation of the entities concerned, e.g. a formal manner of customer service, such as the provision of investment advisory services or the receipt and transfer of orders for the purchase or disposal of financial instruments. There is a regulation on the reporting of investment fund associations to the Financial Supervision Commission. Implementing act on technical measures and conditions for the transmission of information to committees. Summary: The draft law amending the Investment Funds Act and some other laws of the Sejm addressed to the Committee on Public Finances for consideration. The Act is intended to ensure supervision of alternative investment funds and the implementation of European Union rules. The aim of the amendment is to increase the security of investors and counterparties in these funds. Kukiz'15 Poselski Club has reviewed its position on the contested law. The bill introduces EU regulations into Polish law, i.e.: - the Alternative Investment Fund Managers Directive (the AIFM Directive) concerning inter alia: defining the way and scope of these funds' activities, - Directive on the coordination of laws, regulations and administrative provisions relating to undertakings for collective investment in transferable securities (UCITS) where there are regulations specifying the tasks and obligations of depositaries, such as liability in the event of the loss of financial instruments. The government bill amending the Act on Public Offering and the Conditions for Introducing Financial Instruments to Organized Trading and Public Companies and Certain Other Acts on 4 December 2015 was addressed to first reading in the Committee on Public Finance. Print No 70 also contains draft implementing acts. The draft law provides for the implementation of European Union law. Most important changes: The amendment has been made to determine what the public offer is (Art. 3. It specifies where the funds are to influence the recording of shares (Art. 5a). There are also new records, such as: An entity authorised in the Member State concerned to apply for such authorisation Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2. The designer added the so-called. the issuer's registration document (Art. 22 (a) to distinguish it from a tender document, i.e. information on securities to be offered to the public or admitted to trading on a regulated market. It is critical to look at the unfulfilled request, which was written as a discrepancy during the consultation, so-called. agreement conference of 27 February 2015 The case concerns the definition of indirect acquisition of shares. The State Treasury has been exempted from the obligation to disclose the holding of shares of public companies. On the one hand, it is an asset for a public shareholder, and on the other hand, the lack of a full picture for other buyers of financial instruments. The argument raised by, inter alia, KNF and investor associations are the fact that all shareholders must have the same rigour on the capital market and that all trading participants should have the same rights. In summary, the draft law was widely consulted and met with numerous comments from representatives of the Polish National Bank, the Polish Financial Supervision Authority, the General Data Protection Officer, the Warsaw Stock Exchange SA, the National Securities Deposit S.A., the Chamber of Brokerage Houses, the Stock Exchange Issuers Association and the Association of Individual Investors. Most of the comments have been clarified or applied in the work on the Law. The Court of First Instance pointed out that it had not been applied in the law - and this was included in one of the Directives (2004/108/EC) - liability for breach of obligations without grossness. Point 39 of the proposal is not sufficiently precise. One could involve encouraging the acquisition or entry of securities and the other could include other behaviours covered by the proposed prohibition.

The subcommittee presented in its report a harmonised proposal covering the issues of both primary projects. It must be stressed with the utmost recognition that the project presented today is a project of good legislation, followed in normal mode, without undue haste. The project was subject to a number of consultations and many financial market institutions participated in the work on the project in both committees and subcommittees. Thanks to this process, many ambiguities were explained, errors of order and legislation were removed and changes were made to adapt the requirements of the EU directives to the conditions of our Polish financial market. The draft law is the implementation of EU law by several EU directives. I will list the existing directives as they define the thematic scope of the project.: - Directive 2011/61/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 8 June 2011 on alternative investment fund managers and amending Directives 2003/41/EC and 2009/65/EC and several Regulations, - additionally Directive 2014/91/EU of 23 July 2014 amending Directive 2009/65/EC on the coordination of laws, regulations and administrative provisions relating to undertakings for collective investment in transferable securities as regards the functions of depositary, remuneration policy and sanctions. The main objective of the law is to increase investor confidence in funds through, among others, strengthening the requirements concerning the obligations and liability of depositaries, the remuneration policy of management companies and investment companies, and the introduction of sanctions due to breaches of obligations by the company's authorities. The changes made give the possibility to monitor and monitor the risks involved, reduce the risks associated with investment in funds, and increase the security of the assets of the funds managed. The proposed changes also enable alternative investment funds based in other Member States to be managed and national alternative investment funds to be offered participation rights in other Member States. In addition, the definition of the parent country was clarified and the procedure for the issuer of that country and the notification of that choice were defined. Much of the changes proposed by a number of financial institutions have been taken into account in the course of the work on the project, and the overarching objective of the change was to increase the competitiveness of our financial market. Particular attention was paid to the draft penalty rates adopted in accordance with Union legislation. These rates were too excessive, inadequate for our Polish capital market. Keeping this size could help to reduce the competitiveness of our capital market and the interest of investors in securities investments. The reduction in rates was one of the main proposals made by parliamentarians and was welcomed by companies participating in the financial market. In conclusion, it must be concluded that the draft law meets the objectives set out in the implementation of EU law and introduces a rational proportion of solutions for our capital market. The Public Finance Commission unanimously adopted the bill and my club, PSL, will also vote in favour of this project.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u120	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! I have the honour to present, on behalf of the applicants, Members of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform, the draft law amending the Act on personal income tax and the Act on income of local government units. The need to amend the Individual Income Tax Act is apparent directly from the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 29 October 2015. The Constitutional Tribunal held that Article. The 27th Individual Income Tax Act setting income tax rates is incompatible with Article. Article 2 and Article 2 84 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland in so far as it does not provide for a mechanism to correct the amount reducing the tax, i.e. the so-called. a tax-free amount to a minimum of existence. The Court held that that provision would, in that respect, expire on 30 November 2016. The revenue from the tax on natural persons is the revenue of the state budget and the budgets of local government units. The change in the tax scale contained in the Act on Income Tax on Individuals therefore requires simultaneous amendments to the Act on Income of Local Government Units. The share of personal income tax is an important part of the revenue of self-government units. The loss of PIT revenue resulting from the implementation of the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal requires compensation on the part of the revenue of local governments. An increase in the amount of freedom without compensation for local governments would mean a radical depletion of most self-government units, and for hundreds of them would be tantamount to practical bankruptcy. On 29 November 2015, the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda presented to the Sejm the bill amending the Act on income tax on individuals - on 2 December last year, the plenary of the Sejm held the first reading of this project, which was addressed to work in the Committee on Public Finance - but this project does not actually implement the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal, nor does it contain any mechanism to compensate for lost revenue to local governments. The judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 28 October 2015 points to the need to adjust the tax reduction amount to the current minimum of existence rate, but also to the need to create a legal mechanism to ensure that also in the future, as a result of price increases, there will not be a situation where the tax-free amount indirectly defined in the Individual Income Tax Act will be lower than the annual minimum of existence rate. The current minimum level of existence laid down by the Institute of Labour and Social Affairs for staff holdings is PLN 544 per month, i.e. PLN 6529 per year. On the other hand, the income criteria set out in the Social Assistance Act should be considered as a legal poverty line, precisely in Article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of this bill. This criterion is the amount of monthly income to which the person is entitled to social assistance cash benefits. Currently, the income criterion for a single economy person is determined by a regulation of the Council of Ministers, i.e. 634 PLN. PLN 7608 per year. The bill proposes the amount of tax reduction equal to the amount proposed in the bill notified by the President. This corresponds to the tax exemption of PLN 8000 per year, i.e. amounts slightly higher than the annual equivalent of the income criterion, I remind you - PLN 7608. The Constitutional Tribunal points to the need to create a lasting guarantee that the amount of tax reduction will retain its real value and, above all, will no longer fall below the minimum of existence not only when the law is adopted but also in subsequent years of application of its provisions. The bill presented to the High Sejm by the President of the Republic contains an increase of the tax-free amount to 8000, but does not contain any mechanism to increase the amount free in subsequent years. Thus, it does not actually implement the ruling of the Constitutional Court, since it does not prevent a recurrence of the real value of the amount free below the minimum of existence. The solutions proposed by the applicants - Members of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - consist in the quota determination of the tax reduction amount in the first year of the new regulation and the introduction of a mechanism to correct this amount in the following years on the basis of the price increase rate of goods and services announced by the President of the Central Statistical Office. However, the most important drawback of the project presented by President Andrzej Duda is the lack of rules to compensate for the negative effects of an increase in tax-free amounts for local governments. In the explanatory memorandum to the presidential project, the applicant estimates that the local authorities will lose about PLN 7 billion a year, but does not propose any compensation in return. The applicant indicates in the explanatory memorandum that the expected effect of the increase in the free amount will be to increase the level of consumption and, consequently, the budgetary revenue, mainly due to VAT. Clearly, however, these revenues will not constitute any compensation for local authorities, as the legislator did not foresee the participation of local authorities in VAT. However, the increase in the free version proposed by the President is down to a very significant increase of around 20%. and a permanent reduction in the level of revenues of local governments, those income from shares in PIT receipts. Shares in income tax revenues from individuals are the largest source of local government's own income. In 2014, they accounted for more than 18% of total revenue and more than 35% of local government's own income. For municipalities, this percentage of PIT income in relation to total own income amounts to more than 33%, for powiats - more than 51%, for cities on the rights of the county - more than 37%, for voivodships - slightly more than 17%. In practice, therefore, all local governments would suffer loss as a result of the adoption of the law in the version prepared by the President's Office. Thus Warsaw would lose about 900 million PLN per year, Poznań - about 190 million PLN per year, Wrocław - over 210 million PLN per year, Gdańsk - 140 million PLN per year. They would also, of course, lose smaller municipalities and cities, e.g. Radom - PLN 45 million per year, Legionowo - PLN 13 million per year, or e.g. the powiat wołomiński - 13 mln zł per year. The introduction of amendments to the Individual Income Tax Act without any compensation adversely affecting the local government of change means a significant reduction in the revenue of the local government and a deterioration of its state and the conditions and possibilities for these local authorities to perform public tasks. This will also result in a reduction in the autonomy and autonomy of local authorities in their financial aspects. It should also be noted that the loss of revenue of local government is to take place without changing the scope of their tasks. This means a significant reduction in the investment capacity of local governments and a radical reduction in the financing of investments from own resources. As a result, it can happen. either to significantly reduce the level of investment expenditure, including the use of European funds, or to significantly increase the debt of local government units. For many local governments, however, a 20% reduction in income from participation in PIT means practical bankruptcy. The budget revenue of these local authorities will be lower than their current expenditure. The sustainability of budgets in their case would have to be linked to the abandonment of some of their own tasks not only by reducing the level of investment, but also, for example, by the liquidation of kindergartens, libraries, cultural houses, part of social benefits or co-financing of public transport. All of these circumstances lead to the conviction that the amendment to the Individual Income Tax Act leading to a reduction in local government income should be accompanied by other statutory amendments compensating local government for the loss of already planned revenues. The simplest solution should be the link with the change in the Individual Income Tax Act at the same time as the amendment in the Local Government Income Act, increasing the share of local governments in tax receipts to the extent that an increased percentage of the share in tax revenues compensates for the reduction in the overall volume of revenue. When preparing the bill, we took into account the positions of the local government organisation. They were equally critical of the draft law presented by the President of the Republic because of the lack of compensation resulting from the increase in the tax reduction amount. And so the Polish County Union in its position of 15 December writes: We remind you that according to the article. 167 of the constitutions of local government units are provided to participate in public income according to their respective tasks, so significant changes - writes the union of powiats - in the Act on personal income tax should be correlated with the changes in the Act on income of local government units, which was without question in the submitted project. And also part of the position of the Polish City Association: The most fair way of compensation would be to increase the share of local governments in PIT revenues, because the actual compensation would occur exactly where the loss occurred. The Rural Union also calls for a simultaneous amendment of the law on the income of local government units. I quote: It is necessary to increase the share of JST in PIT tax accordingly or to transfer part of the revenue of the state budget from indirect taxes, such as VAT or excise duty, to the extent that it compensates for the planned loss of revenue. Opinion of the Rural Union of 23 December 2015 We therefore propose in the submitted project an increase in the percentage shares of local government units corresponding to the total reduction of the amount of tax revenue by changing the percentages used separately for municipalities, powiats and voivodships in Article. 4, Article 5 and Article 5 6 Act on the income of local government units and formulas for the calculation of amounts for individual units regulated by Article. Article 9 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 One-third of the Act on the Income of Individuals. Since the need to compensate for the full amount will only occur in 2018, it is proposed to introduce a transitional provision by adding an article to the Act. 89a determining the percentage of individual categories of self-governments in 2017 corresponding to the reduction of the overall income from personal tax. As a result, the following percentage of the shares of local government units in income tax revenues from individuals are proposed: for municipalities, the share is intended to be 48,82%. , for powiats - 12,82% and for voivodships - 1,97%. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The bill presented has two objectives. Firstly, the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal on how to calculate the tax reduction amount, and secondly, the protection of the income of local government units against the negative effects of the necessary amendments to the Individual Income Tax Act. The amendments proposed in the Individual Income Act are derived from changes in the Individual Income Tax Act, as the aim of the proposed project is not, in fact, to change the level of revenue of local governments, but to maintain their current level to an extent not threatened by legislative changes resulting from the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal. With this in mind, I ask the High House to decide whether to continue to proceed with this bill and to continue to work in the parliamentary committees. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u126	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I present my position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on Income Tax on Individuals and the Act on Income of Local Government Units from Print No 221. Secondly, it introduces solutions to compensate local government units for the loss of their share of personal income tax. The current presidential project and project assume a tax reduction of PLN 1440 which corresponds to the tax exemption of PLN 8 000. PLN per year. For the calculation of these amounts, the indicator resulting from the income criterion authorising the receipt of cash benefits from social assistance, which appears to be a better option than the application of the minimum existence index, was used. At the same time, the judgment of the Constitutional Court indicates that the solution to this problem should be permanent. This amount will be adjusted annually precisely according to the price increase rate of consumer goods and services calculated for the first half of the year preceding the tax year in relation to the same period of the previous year. The draft law also lays down the rules for rounding off the amount paid and requires the Minister responsible for public finances to publish annually, by 30 September, the amount reducing the income tax in the following year. In the presidential project, a solution resulting in a loss of revenue of the state budget was justified by the so-called "state budget'. the multiplier effect, i.e. the increase in consumption demand, the increase in revenue from the tax on goods and services, including business tax, and thus the possibility of secondary recovery of these revenues. Unfortunately, similar compensation will not exist in the case of write-downs for municipalities, powiats and voivodships. Hence, the projecters propose to increase the target share of income from individuals in municipalities - from 39,34% to 48,46% , powiats - from 10,25% to 12,63%, and voivodships - from 1,6% to 1.97% , except that in 2017 this indicator will amount to 1.95% for voivodships. This year 2017 is a transitional period and the amendments proposed in the Local Government Income Act are derived from changes in the Individual Income Tax Act. Thus, the aim of the bill is not to change, but to maintain the current level of revenue of local government. We are in favour of directing this project to further work in committees. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 26 July 1991 on income tax on individuals setting income tax rates in so far as it does not provide for a mechanism to correct the tax reduction amount guaranteeing at least the minimum existence, with Article. Article 2 and Article 2 84 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland. At the same time, the Constitutional Tribunal stated in its judgment that that provision would expire to the extent indicated on 30 November 2016. It should also be noted at this point that their statutory draft amendments to the regulation of the amount were submitted by the Modern Posel Club and the President of the Republic of Andrzej Duda. It is worth noting that print No 221 refers to two very important and interrelated issues, namely the increase of the tax-free amount and the resulting loss of revenue of local government units, whose budget depends to a large extent on these revenues. PLN when costs reach the level of PLN 8 billion on the part of the state budget and PLN 8 billion on the part of local government units? After reading the print No 221 submitted by Members of the Civic Platform, the Modern Club makes the following comments to it: 1. In this draft law you have written down an amount which reduces the tax permanently in the amount of PLN 1440, while at the same time writing down a way of correcting the amount which is not sufficiently precise. Ladies and gentlemen, this is where we make the law. Do you want to create a wrong law that allows for different interpretations, or do you want to create a clear and understandable law? Modern in its project proposed a method of calculation based on variable algorithm, which will allow to increase the amount free of income tax in a predictable way for the following years. 2. The calculation of the income tax-free amount for individuals determined on the basis of the CPI, i.e. the price increase rate of consumer goods and services, so-called. the consumer inflation index. This indicator is calculated monthly by the Central Statistical Office. However, as the National Chamber of Auditors points out in its opinion, the use of the CPI may lead to difficulties in estimating revenue and instability in constructing the budgets of local government units. In a similar tone, the Civic Platform Commission also speaks of the proposed law notified. Public Finances of the Association of Voivodships of the Republic of Poland. 3. While remaining using the CPI, according to the Modern Club, it will not be fully objective, as will the minimum existence rate defined by the Institute of Labour and Social Affairs. In the project presented by the club, the modern amount depends on a minimum of existence index, which will be more meaningful. 4. The issue of the participation of local government units in these revenues, which constitute a significant part of the budget of the individuals mentioned, is extremely important in the construction of laws on taxes on individuals. As in its opinions, it notes both the Committee on the Environment and Climate Action. public finances, as well as the National Chamber of Auditors as a result of the proposed change of local government units will lose a significant part of their own income, and additional concern is the fact that the differences are to be covered by subsidies. Such a solution, in the absence of changes in the method of calculating subsidies, i.e. the calculation of subsidies unchanged, will result in a real reduction in the value of the budgets of local government units. 5. It is also worth noting that the percentage share proposed by the Civic Platform in income does not offset the real losses that local governments will suffer as a result of changes in the law, as indicated above. The Civic Platform assumes that the percentage share should increase over time, which will not compensate for the reduction of tax revenue. It is also worth mentioning the proposed values. The platform calls for an increase in the share of the tax for municipalities to 48,82% in the end. , powiats - 12,82% and voivodships - to 1,97%. Really looking at your proposals, we already know that they will lead to a reduction in government revenue from PIT tax. Members of the Civic Platform, do you want local governments not to be able to carry out their tasks because of lack of resources? Modern assumes an increase in the share of PIT income to 49,9% for municipalities, 13.6% for powiats and 2,12% for voivodships, which will keep the level of income from PIT tax for local government units at the current level. In view of the above, I refer on behalf of the Modern Club to refer the project after first reading to committee work.

Poznań June was the first mass revolt of workers and inhabitants of such a large city in the Polish People's Republic. Students of the University of Technology in Budapest organized a demonstration to support the demands of Poznań workers, whose aim was to express solidarity with the Polish people. The slogan was chanted: Poland shows the way... ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u192	Dear Marshals! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to present to you a law that farmers have been waiting for for many years, a law that farmers have been fighting for. They demand from us, Members, from us, the rulers, to present such a bill. The Act is supposed to be short, and only a few paragraphs, amendments, amendments, changes, but it is a law that completely changes the system of covering the costs of losses caused by wild game, a law that will change the system, as well as a good change for farmers. But she is also accepted, which we are very pleased with, by hunters. So that this is a solution that solves a very big problem, the problem primarily concerning our farmers. I will give you some insight into the changes that we are proposing in this law. The first thing is the conduct by the voivode of proceedings for the estimation and compensation of damage caused by wild boars, elks, deer, dandels and deer and damage caused by hunting. It is the voivode who will appoint a committee to estimate the damage. This is the third element that will be introduced in this entire injury assessment process. So far, hunting wheels have done this in the presence of the farmer. At the moment there will be a committee - the third element, the most objective one - which will estimate the damage. The representatives of the agricultural chambers, of course the owner of the land, i.e. the farmer, and the manager of the hunting circuit, will also be provided to participate in the inspection and assessment of damage upon written request. It will be possible for those who raise objections to the minutes of the opinion of the assessor included in the list of experts led by the competent authority in view of the place of injury to the agricultural chamber. The funds for reimbursement of the costs of drawing up expert opinions would come from the compensation fund. The decision on compensation issued by the voivode is to be final and the owner or holder of the agricultural land on which the damage was caused by the hunting, tenant or manager of the hunting circuit is not satisfied with that decision may, within one month of its date of service, bring an action before the regional court. The creation of a State Compensation Fund is a second very important change. Firstly, it estimates the losses of the committee appointed by the voivode and the second element in this amendment is the creation of a State Compensation Fund. The manager of this fund will be the voivode responsible for the area of damage and the payment of compensation for hunting damages from that fund, mainly financed by annual contributions from tenants or managers of hunting circuits. Technically, it will look like this, that hunting circles will pay a year's contribution to the fund - I will tell you exactly what contribution we offer in our bill, in our concept of changes, how much - and the rest will be done by the voivode. This voivode will, as I said, estimate the damage and the voivode will pay the funds that we collect from the contributions. The contributions will be the same as at the moment, about the same limits, maybe a little higher, but they will secure the payment of compensation not only at the same amount as before. Right now, ladies and gentlemen, the average... Or maybe otherwise: all compensation according to the CSO paid in 2014 amounted to PLN 70 million. We estimate that the contributions collected will amount to PLN 100 million. There will therefore be 30 million more contributions than at the moment is the amount of compensation paid. So if there's more damage, and we know that they're going to be higher, because so far there's been a lot of charges in their estimation, we still have 30 million at this point, so let's call it yes, a backup so that these compensations can be higher. But if these funds were not enough, the hunting circles would no longer be paying more contributions, the rest of the money, the missing ones, if this were the case, would be paid out of the state budget. It is a security to pay the compensation that will be due to farmers. A year's contribution. The annual contribution paid by hunters by hunting wheels will depend on the size of the circuit. We estimate that the maximum annual contribution per hectare will be equivalent to 10 kg of rye. As I have told you before, our calculations show that this will be within the limit of PLN 100 million per year. In conclusion, I would like to ask you to go to the agricultural committee for this bill. Your Marshal, this is a formal proposal for him to be included in the Committee on Agriculture in addition to the Committee on the Environment, so that we can debate it together. If there are any comments, and we know that some comments are made in the opinions on some provisions - not on the idea, not on the purpose we define, but on some entries - then I hope that together with the environmental committee at the meetings we will regulate these matters so that it is the best law and that it best protects the interests of the victims above all, i.e. farmers, but also the interests of hunters, so that it is, as it is said, well negotiated, jointly negotiated, for both sides. ParlaMint-PL_2016-02-25-sejm-12-2.u206	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the revision of the Hunting Law Act. According to the Act of 13 October 1995, the Law of Hunting Animals, i.e. wild animals, which are allowed to hunt as a national good, is the property of the State Treasury, so the subject of their protection in the context of the hunting economy should be subject to extensive public consultation, which is not limited only to farmers, hunters and foresters and their interests. The same legal provisions indicate that the hunting animals legally obtained in the hunting area are transferred to the property of the tenant or the manager of the hunting circuit, which are usually hunting wheels and the Polish Hunting Union. It's an express privatisation. In exchange for the ownership of the State, these entities are obliged to pay compensation to farmers for damage caused by wild boars, elks, deer, dandels and deer, as well as damage caused by the hunting itself. Nevertheless, farmers complain that compensation for hunting damages is paid at a low level and too late since the damage occurred. Farmers often also consider that their damage is underestimated. It happens that farmers are bankrupt pending compensation for the destruction of crops. The amendment of the law requires owners or holders of agricultural land to cooperate in securing land against damage. In the event of a dispute over the amount of compensation for damage, the parties may refer to the municipality authority for mediation and amicable settlement of the dispute, which is a big advantage of this amendment. The main objective of the amendment to Hunting Law is, however, to partially transfer the obligation to pay hunting damages to the voivodeship State Treasury acting on behalf of the State Treasury, and consequently to co-finance the activities of hunters by citizens and citizens of the Republic of Poland. It should be noted that hunters represent only 0.3% the population, of which 63% Poles declare their lack of acceptance for hunting. According to the amendment, a compensation fund is to be created, the resources of which will be the sum of annual contributions paid by the tenants or managers of hunting circuits, donations and entries, grants from the State budget and other revenue received for the Fund. The project lacks clear mechanisms to assess the burden on the State Treasury. The projector assumes an annual regulatory cost of 55 million, of which 30 million are funds for the compensation fund, and as much as 25 million are the costs of voivodeships and as many as 380 new jobs. The project presents a lack of safeguards to protect the state budget against these expenditures. Here we see another solution - setting the annual premium for the tenants or managers of hunting circuits at the price of 0.1 q rye for 1 ha of the hunting circuit would cover compensation and the cost of paying them without charging the State Treasury. In 2015, hunting wheels leased hunting circuits with an area of 25 250 thousand. ha, the President of the Central Statistical Office announced that the average buying-in price for rye for the 11 quarters preceding the fiscal year 2016 is PLN 53,75 per quintal, so that the proceeds from the contributions of tenants or managers of hunting circuits may amount to as much as PLN 136 million. The amount of compensation paid in leased and managed circuits by the Polish Łowiecki Union in the years 2014 to 2015 amounted to 62 150 thousand. zł. Hunting circles and the Polish Hunting Union currently derive considerable financial benefits from the sale of ink and the organization of commercial hunting. According to statistics of the Central Statistical Office, the value of buying game in Poland is PLN 81 million. In addition, hunting is an additional form of fundraising for hunters. Two types of hunting are legally carried out in Poland: individual and collective. To more than 118,000. registered in the Polish Hunting Union hunters join tens of thousands of foreigners participating in foreign exchange hunts annually. A relatively liberal hunting law that accepts trophy supporters is decisive in this regard. Supervision of this type of activity is quite illusory, as shown by the audit carried out by the Supreme Audit Chamber. Research also shows that there is a direct correlation between feeding game and hunting damage. Changes in the Hunting Law are needed, the problem of compensation is important, and the harm to farmers - evidently, however, we are in favour of a different approach to this issue.
